# Charlie Kirk

This article is about the American activist. For other people named Charlie or Charles Kirk, see [Charles Kirk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Kirk_\(disambiguation\)).

**Charles James  Kirk** (October 14, 1993 – September 10, 2025) was an American [right-wing](56522.md) political activist, entrepreneur, and [media personality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_personality). He co‑founded the [conservative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservatism_in_the_United_States) student organization [Turning Point USA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turning_Point_USA) (TPUSA) in 2012 and served as its executive director until [his assassination](81040139.md) in 2025. A key ally of [Donald Trump](4848272.md), he became one of the most prominent voices of the [MAGA movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MAGA_movement) within the [Republican Party](32070.md), publishing several books and hosting *[The Charlie Kirk Show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Charlie_Kirk_Show)*.

Born and raised in the [Chicago suburbs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_suburbs) of [Arlington Heights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arlington_Heights,_Illinois) and [Prospect Heights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prospect_Heights,_Illinois), Kirk became politically active in high school. He briefly attended college before dropping out to focus on building TPUSA with political donor [Bill Montgomery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_Montgomery_\(activist\)). The project later expanded into affiliate groups such as [Turning Point Action](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turning_Point_Action) and [Turning Point Faith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turning_Point_Faith). Kirk's activism centered on mobilizing conservative students and promoting Republican causes nationwide.

Kirk advocated for a range of conservative positions, including opposition to [abortion](765.md), [gun control](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gun_politics_in_the_United_States), [DEI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diversity,_equity,_and_inclusion) programs, and [LGBTQ rights](3510770.md). Over time, he aligned with the [Christian right](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_right) and advocated for [Christian nationalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_nationalism_in_the_United_States). His more controversial positions included criticism of the [Civil Rights Act of 1964](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_Rights_Act_of_1964) and [Martin Luther King Jr.](20076.md), as well as promotion of [COVID-19 misinformation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_misinformation), [false allegations of electoral fraud in 2020](65905202.md), and the [white genocide conspiracy theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_genocide_conspiracy_theory).

On September 10, 2025, Kirk was fatally shot  by a rooftop sniper while speaking at a TPUSA debate event at [Utah Valley University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Utah_Valley_University). His assassination drew international attention and condemnation of [political violence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_violence), while also sparking partisan dispute. On September 21, almost 100,000 people attended [his memorial service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memorial_service_of_Charlie_Kirk) at [State Farm Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Farm_Stadium) in [Glendale, Arizona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glendale,_Arizona).

## Early life

Charles James Kirk was born on October 14, 1993, in the [Chicago](6886.md) suburb of [Arlington Heights, Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arlington_Heights,_Illinois), to Robert Willard Kirk and Kathryn Smith Kirk ([née](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Birth_name#Maiden_and_married_names) Smith),[^cite_note-NYT_Obit-1] and raised in the nearby city of [Prospect Heights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prospect_Heights,_Illinois).[^cite_note-keilman-2] His father is an architect who was involved in the construction of [Trump Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trump_Tower).[^cite_note-Beaumont_2025-3][^cite_note-Independent9.14.25-4] His mother[^cite_note-5] is a former trader at the [Chicago Mercantile Exchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Mercantile_Exchange) who subsequently worked as a mental health counselor.[^cite_note-keilman-2][^cite_note-Beaumont_2025-3][^cite_note-Independent9.14.25-4] He had one younger sister, Mary, who went on to become an [art curator](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art_curator) in Chicago.[^cite_note-\:0-6][^cite_note-7]

Kirk described his parents as [moderate Republicans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moderates_in_the_Republican_Party_\(United_States\)).[^cite_note-keilman-2] They were active in conservative circles, and his father was a major donor to the [Mitt Romney 2012 presidential campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mitt_Romney_2012_presidential_campaign).[^cite_note-NYT_Obit-1] Raised in the [Presbyterian Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presbyterian_Church), Kirk was a member of the [Boy Scouts of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boy_Scouts_of_America) and earned the rank of [Eagle Scout](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eagle_Scout).[^cite_note-JM-8][^cite_note-Manzhos2025-9] He experienced a political awakening in middle school, during which he read books by economist [Milton Friedman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milton_Friedman) and became more attracted to the principles of the Republican Party.[^cite_note-keilman-2]

In 2010, during his junior year at [Wheeling High School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wheeling_High_School), Kirk volunteered for the successful [U.S. Senate campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_United_States_Senate_elections_in_Illinois) of Illinois Republican [Mark Kirk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Kirk) (no relation).[^cite_note-10] Also during his junior year, he began listening to *[The Rush Limbaugh Show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Rush_Limbaugh_Show)*, a prominent [conservative talk radio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservative_talk_radio) broadcast.[^cite_note-NYT_Obit-1] In his senior year, he initiated a boycott of cookies at the school's cafeteria to reverse a price increase.[^cite_note-keilman-2] He also wrote an essay for *[Breitbart News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Breitbart_News)* alleging liberal bias in high-school textbooks; it led to his first media appearance on [Fox Business](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fox_Business) at age 17.[^cite_note-Smith_2025-11][^cite_note-Bim-12]

In 2012, Kirk applied to [West Point](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Military_Academy) but was rejected.[^cite_note-Smith_2025-11][^cite_note-Bim-12] Although he was accepted that same year to [Baylor University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baylor_University) in [Waco, Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waco,_Texas), he enrolled instead at [Harper College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harper_College), a [community college](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Community_college) in [Palatine, Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palatine,_Illinois). Withdrawing after one [semester](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academic_term), he left Harper to concentrate on his work with Turning Point USA, the political group he co-founded with conservative businessman and mentor [Bill Montgomery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_Montgomery_\(activist\)).[^cite_note-NYT_Obit-1][^cite_note-Smith_2025-11] In 2015, Kirk enrolled part-time at [King's College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King's_College_\(New_York_City\)) in New York City, taking online classes.[^cite_note-atlantic-13] Kirk did not receive a college degree during his lifetime, a fact he noted in debates with academics and students.[^cite_note-BBCNews9.21.25-14]

## Organizations

### Turning Point USA

Main article: [Turning Point USA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turning_Point_USA)



Kirk speaking at the 2013 [Conservative Political Action Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservative_Political_Action_Conference) (CPAC) in [National Harbor, Maryland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Harbor,_Maryland)

In May 2012, Kirk gave a speech at [Benedictine University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benedictine_University)'s "Youth Government Day", where he met [Bill Montgomery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_Montgomery_\(activist\)), who was then a [Tea Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tea_Party_movement)-backed legislative candidate.[^cite_note-atlantic-13][^cite_note-15] Montgomery later said that the event's speakers had bored the audience of a few hundred high-school kids, but the students began paying rapt attention when Kirk started speaking. Montgomery encouraged Kirk to pursue political activism full time.[^cite_note-atlantic-13][^cite_note-NYT_Obit-1] A month after they first met, Montgomery and Kirk co-founded Turning Point USA, aiming to create an organization that would rival [liberal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modern_liberalism_in_the_United_States) groups such as [MoveOn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MoveOn).[^cite_note-Bykowicz-16][^cite_note-atlantic-13] Kirk described it as a student organization advocating for free markets and limited government.[^cite_note-PBSNewshour9.11.25-17] At the [2012 Republican National Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Republican_National_Convention), Kirk met [Foster Friess](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foster_Friess), a former investment manager and prominent Republican donor, and persuaded him to finance the organization.[^cite_note-atlantic-13][^cite_note-Bykowicz-16]

Kirk remained the executive director, chief fundraiser, and the public face of Turning Point USA until his death in 2025.[^cite_note-Guinto-18][^cite_note-JM-8][^cite_note-PBSNewshour9.11.25-17] He became known for visiting college campuses to host informal debates with ideological opponents, typically students, and attempting to persuade them to consider conservative candidates.[^cite_note-19] He often sat behind a table bearing his slogan, "Prove me Wrong".[^cite_note-PBSNewshour9.11.25-17] According to the [Associated Press](18935732.md), video clips of Kirk's campus appearances spread online, helping him "secure a steady stream of donations that transformed Turning Point into one of the country's largest political organizations". Turning Point eventually began holding massive rallies in which top conservative leaders addressed tens of thousands of young voters.[^cite_note-PBSNewshour9.11.25-17] In 2025, TPUSA said it had chapters at more than 2,000 college and high school campuses, and that it had received 32,000 inquiries about starting new chapters in the days after Kirk's death.[^cite_note-20]

TPUSA's activities include publication of the [Professor Watchlist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Professor_Watchlist) and the School Board Watchlist.[^cite_note-21] Critics of these watchlists say they threaten academic freedom and have led to the targeted harassment of academics.[^cite_note-22][^cite_note-23] In 2019, the Professor Watchlist was briefly suspended by its web host.[^cite_note-24] In 2020, [ProPublica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ProPublica) investigated TPUSA's finances and found that the organization made "misleading financial claims" and that its audits were not done by an independent auditor. ProPublica also reported that Kirk's salary from TPUSA had increased from \$27,000 to nearly \$300,000 and that he had bought an \$855,000 condo in [Longboat Key, Florida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Longboat_Key,_Florida).[^cite_note-25] In 2020, Turning Point USA had \$39.2 million in revenue.[^cite_note-26] Kirk earned a salary of more than \$325,000 from TPUSA and related organizations.[^cite_note-PostVaccine-27]

#### Turning Point Academy

In 2021, TPUSA announced it would launch an online academy as an alternative to schools "poisoning our youth with anti-American ideas". Turning Point Academy was intended to cater to families seeking an "America-first education".[^cite_note-Stanley-Becker_&_Reinhard_2022-28] Arizona education firm StrongMind initially partnered with TPUSA with plans to open the academy by the fall of 2022 and assessed its "potential to generate over \$40 million in gross revenue at full capacity (10,000 students)".[^cite_note-Stanley-Becker_&_Reinhard_2022-28] The partnership ended after StrongMind received backlash from its own employees, and key subcontractor Freedom Learning Group, which prepared course content for the academy, also backed out.[^cite_note-Stanley-Becker_&_Reinhard_2022-28] In 2022, Turning Point partnered with Dream City Christian School, a private school that has campuses in Glendale and [Scottsdale, Arizona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scottsdale,_Arizona), and is affiliated with [Dream City Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dream_City_Church).[^cite_note-host-29] In the 2022–2023 school year, the school received \$900,000 in Arizona school voucher funds.[^cite_note-host-29]

### Turning Point Action

Main article: [Turning Point Action](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turning_Point_Action)



Kirk and then-former President [Donald Trump](4848272.md) speaking with attendees at a Turning Point Action Conference in 2023

In May 2019, it was reported that Kirk was preparing to launch Turning Point Action, a [501(c)(4)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/501\(c\)\(4\)) designed to elect more conservatives.[^cite_note-30] In July 2019, Kirk announced that Turning Point Action had acquired Students for Trump along with "all associated media assets".[^cite_note-31] He became chairman and launched a campaign to mobilize the youth vote for the 2020 Trump reelection campaign.[^cite_note-Smith_2025-11] The unsuccessful effort led TPUSA and the [2020 Trump campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Trump_campaign) to blame each other for an overall decline in Trump's youth support.[^cite_note-Orr-2020-32] In December 2022, Kirk announced the Mount Vernon Project, an initiative by Turning Point Action to remove members from the [Republican National Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republican_National_Committee) who were not "grassroot conservatives".[^cite_note-Washington_Post-33]

On January 5, 2021, the day before the Washington, D.C., protest that led to the [January 6 U.S. Capitol attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/January_6_U.S._Capitol_attack), Kirk wrote on Twitter that Turning Point Action and Students for Trump were sending more than 80 "buses of patriots to D.C. to fight for this president".[^cite_note-Qiu-2020-34][^cite_note-dailydot2021-01-10-35] A spokesman for Turning Point said that the groups ended up sending seven buses with 350 students.[^cite_note-Qiu-2020-34][^cite_note-36] In the lead-up to the storming, Kirk said he was "getting 500 emails a minute calling for a civil war".[^cite_note-37] [Publix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Publix) heiress [Julie Fancelli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julie_Fancelli) gave Kirk's organizations \$1.25 million to fund the buses to the January 6 event. Kirk also paid \$60,000 for [Kimberly Guilfoyle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kimberly_Guilfoyle) to speak at the rally.[^cite_note-38]

Afterward, Kirk "said the violent acts at the Capitol were not an insurrection and did not represent mainstream Trump supporters."[^cite_note-Bim-12][^cite_note-39][^cite_note-40] Appearing before the [U.S. House Select Committee on the January 6 Attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._House_Select_Committee_on_the_January_6_Attack) in December 2022, he pleaded the [Fifth Amendment privilege against self-incrimination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fifth_Amendment_privilege_against_self-incrimination). His team provided the committee "with 8,000 pages of records in response to its requests".[^cite_note-41] In another closed-door meeting of the House January 6 Committee, [Ali Alexander](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ali_Alexander) blamed Kirk and TPUSA for financing the travel of demonstrators to the [Save America rally](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Save_America_rally).[^cite_note-42] TPUSA spokesperson [Andrew Kolvet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrew_Kolvet) denied that Kirk advocated for violence and gave a statement saying "Charlie wants to save America with words, persuasion, courage and common sense. The left is desperate to conjure up some Christian bogeyman that simply doesn't exist. We're telling churches: Either get involved and have a say in the direction of your country or you'll leave a void that someone else who doesn't share your values will fill."[^cite_note-Hixenbaugh_&_Smith_2024-43]

### Falkirk Center for Faith and Liberty



Kirk and [Candace Owens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Candace_Owens) speaking at the Student Action Summit hosted by Turning Point USA, [West Palm Beach, Florida](109634.md) in December 2018

In November 2019, Kirk and [Jerry Falwell Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jerry_Falwell_Jr.) co-founded the Falkirk Center for Faith and Liberty, a right-wing think tank funded, owned, and housed by [Liberty University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberty_University).[^cite_note-44][^cite_note-graham-45] ("Falkirk" was a [portmanteau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portmanteau) of "Falwell" and "Kirk".)[^cite_note-graham-45] Fellows included Antonia Okafor, director of outreach for [Gun Owners of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gun_Owners_of_America); [Sebastian Gorka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sebastian_Gorka), former deputy assistant to Trump; and [Jenna Ellis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jenna_Ellis), a senior legal counselor for Trump.[^cite_note-46]

In 2020, the Falkirk Center spent at least \$50,000 on Facebook advertisements promoting Trump and Republican candidates.[^cite_note-47] Students and alumni raised objections to the organization's aggressive political tone, which they considered inconsistent with the university's mission.[^cite_note-graham-45] Falwell resigned as president of Liberty University in August 2020, and the university did not renew Kirk's one-year contract in late 2020. In 2021, the university renamed the organization to the Standing for Freedom Center.[^cite_note-graham-45]

### Turning Point Faith

After [Liberty University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberty_University) did not renew Kirk's contract with the Falkirk Center for Faith and Liberty in 2021, Kirk and [Pentecostal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pentecostal) pastor Rob McCoy founded Turning Point Faith, an organization that encouraged pastors and other church leaders to be active in local and national political issues.[^cite_note-graham-45][^cite_note-Potts2024-48] Its activities include faith-based voter drives and promotion of TPUSA's views, with the stated goal to help churches become more civically engaged so that American society can "return to foundational Christian values".[^cite_note-49] According to TPUSA's 2021 Investor Prospectus, the program—with a budget of \$6.4 million—will "address America's crumbling religious foundation by engaging thousands of pastors nationwide" in order to "breathe renewed civic engagement into our churches".[^cite_note-50]

## Media

### Talk shows



Kirk debating with students at the [University of Tennessee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Tennessee) in March 2025

From October 2020 until his assassination, Kirk hosted [The Charlie Kirk Show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Charlie_Kirk_Show), a daily three-hour radio talk show, on [Salem Media Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salem_Media_Group)'s "The Answer" radio channel.[^cite_note-51] It was among the most popular podcasts on Apple Podcasts. According to internal data from TPUSA, Kirk's podcast was downloaded between 500,000 and 750,000 times each day in 2024.[^cite_note-SmithGomez-52] Kirk's "Turning Point Live" was a three-hour streaming talk show aimed at [Generation Z](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Generation_Z_in_the_United_States). TPUSA's monthly online average grew to 111,000 unique visitors in 2021.[^cite_note-53] A February 2023 [Brookings Institution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brookings_Institution) study found Kirk's podcast contained the second-highest proportion of false, misleading, and unsubstantiated statements among 36,603 episodes produced by 79 prominent political podcasters.[^cite_note-54]

In a 2022 episode of his podcast, Kirk called for a "patriot" to bail out of jail the man who broke into [Nancy Pelosi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nancy_Pelosi)'s house and [attacked and tried to murder her husband with a hammer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attack_on_Paul_Pelosi).[^cite_note-55][^cite_note-Beaumont_2025-3] Also in 2022, journalist [Bari Weiss](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bari_Weiss) released a report of internal Twitter documents dubbed "The [Twitter Files](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twitter_Files)", which alleged that Twitter was censoring conservative personalities on the social media platform. Weiss posted screenshots of Twitter tools that moderators could use to limit the reach of posts and accounts. According to *Rolling Stone* magazine, Kirk's Twitter account was flagged under "do not amplify", which meant algorithms would not highlight tweets coming from it.[^cite_note-56][^cite_note-57]

In April 2024, Kirk created a [TikTok](56822861.md) account after previously expressing skepticism of the social media platform. His account gained popularity after he posted numerous videos of himself talking to college students on his campus tours, with some videos garnering as many as 50 million views.[^cite_note-NYTimesYouthWhisperer-58] In February 2025, Kirk signed with the [Trinity Broadcasting Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trinity_Broadcasting_Network) to host a weekday talk show, *Charlie Kirk Today*.[^cite_note-59]

### Debate style

At TPUSA's college events, Kirk often propounded right-wing populist and nationalist views and invited students to "prove \[him] wrong" (stylized in [all caps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All_caps)) in front of an audience of vocal supporters.[^cite_note-BBCNews9.21.25-14] *The New York Times* reviewed more than four dozen debates at Kirk's twice-yearly university tours, and discussed them with four debate coaches and university professors. The analysis showed Kirk using the debate format to create viral confrontations and "deliver a consistent hard-line message while orchestrating highly shareable moments". *The Times* described him as having the advantage of having refined his debate performance against hundreds of people and used arguments likely to result in agitated and defensive responses by his less-experienced opponents.[^cite_note-60]

Kirk was influential within the American conservative movement, particularly among young Christians.[^cite_note-61][^cite_note-62] *[The New York Times](30680.md)* said Kirk symbolized hope for the [Christian right](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_right).[^cite_note-DiasGraham2025-63] From others, Kirk's political activism received criticism.[^cite_note-Smith_2025-11] His rhetoric was described as divisive, racist, [xenophobic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xenophobic), and extreme by groups that studied [hate speech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hate_speech), including the [Southern Poverty Law Center](27164.md). Kirk disagreed with critics who said he created a toxic online environment, arguing: "Disagreement is a healthy part of our systems."[^cite_note-64] Kirk's positions have been described as [far-right](221220.md) by a variety of outlets and academics,[^cite_note-Stone_2024-65][^cite_note-Rudnick_2024-66][^cite_note-67] and others state that these positions are now in the mainstream of American conservatism.[^cite_note-68]

Kirk was the [William F. Buckley Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_F._Buckley_Jr.) council member of the [Council for National Policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_for_National_Policy) (CNP), a group "that has served for decades as a hub for a nationwide network of conservative activists and the donors who support them",[^cite_note-disrupt-69] according to the CNP's September 2020 membership directory leaked in February 2021.[^cite_note-70][^cite_note-shadow-71][^cite_note-rub-72] He was a spokesperson for CNP Action, the political arm of the CNP.[^cite_note-shadow-71] In March 2025, Trump appointed Kirk to the [U.S. Air Force Academy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Air_Force_Academy) Board of Visitors.[^cite_note-73][^cite_note-74] Kirk's last political rally took place in Kentucky, where he appeared alongside U.S. Senate candidate [Nate Morris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nate_Morris).[^cite_note-75][^cite_note-76]

### Republican and pro-Trump activism

In an interview with *[Wired](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wired_\(magazine\))* during the 2016 Republican National Convention, Kirk said that while he "was not the world's biggest Donald Trump fan", he would vote for him, and that Trump's candidacy made Turning Point's mission more difficult.[^cite_note-77] Kirk flipped to supporting Trump at the convention and spent the remainder of the campaign assisting with travel and media arrangements for [Donald Trump Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump_Jr.)[^cite_note-gofer-78] In October 2016, Kirk participated in a [Fox News](11121.md) event along with Trump Jr., [Eric Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eric_Trump), and [Lara Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lara_Trump) that had a pro-Donald Trump tone.[^cite_note-79]

Vice President [JD Vance](53396477.md) recalled that Kirk first reached out to him in 2017, before Vance entered politics, and that Kirk was one of the first people he called when he considered running for Senate. Kirk introduced him to his eventual campaign managers as well as to Donald Trump Jr.[^cite_note-PBSNewshour9.11.25-17] Other notable Republicans also credit Kirk with helping them establish careers in politics.[^cite_note-PBSNewshour9.11.25-17]

In July 2019, Kirk became chairman of [Students for Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Students_for_Trump), which had been acquired by Turning Point Action.[^cite_note-Smith_2025-11] The unsuccessful effort of his youth mobilization campaign led TPUSA and the Trump campaign to blame each other for an overall decline in Trump's youth support.[^cite_note-Orr-2020-32] In April 2020, [Matthew Rosenberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matthew_Rosenberg) and Katie Rogers wrote in *The New York Times* that Kirk exemplifies "walking the line between mainstream conservative opinion and outright [disinformation](142690.md)" and that "with a powerful ally in the president's eldest son, Donald Trump Jr., Mr. Kirk both amplifies the president's message and helps shape it."[^cite_note-gofer-78]

On March 3, 2020, Kirk released his book *The MAGA Doctrine*, a manifesto for the Make America Great Again movement, in which he wrote that the Republican Party is "in some sense no longer a [conservative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservatism_in_the_United_States) party, no longer [the party of Reagan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reagan_era), but instead a Trump-remade populist party".[^cite_note-80] In December 2022, Kirk urged the [Republican National Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republican_National_Committee) to listen to their grassroots voters, saying, "If ignored, we will have the most stunted and muted Republican Party in the history of the conservative movement, the likes of which we haven't seen in generations."[^cite_note-Washington_Post-33] In 2023, Kirk called for the imprisonment or the death penalty of [Joe Biden](145422.md) for "crimes against America".[^cite_note-81] Kirk was an early investor in [1789 Capital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1789_Capital), which invests in MAGA businesses.[^cite_note-82]

Kirk's engagement of young voters is credited with playing a key role in the [2024 U.S. presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_U.S._presidential_election).[^cite_note-83] Before the election, Kirk visited approximately 25 college campuses, marketed as the "You're Being Brainwashed" tour, aiming to stir up more [Gen Z](4015968.md) voter turnout as he engaged and debated students on many topics.[^cite_note-\:0-6] According to Turning Point Action, the tour produced around two billion views on social media.[^cite_note-84] Trump credited Kirk as one of the handful of allies most responsible for his victory;[^cite_note-\:1-85] Vance credited him with not only the Trump win the 2024 presidential election, but with helping to staff the entire government afterward.[^cite_note-PBSNewshour9.11.25-17] Kirk aided Trump in choosing leadership positions for his administration, including cabinet positions.[^cite_note-86]

During 2025, Kirk endorsed a number of Republican candidates, including [Andy Biggs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andy_Biggs) in the Arizona governor contest and [Nate Morris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nate_Morris) in the Kentucky U.S. Senate primary.[^cite_note-87][^cite_note-88] In July 2025, Kirk conducted interviews about [Jeffrey Epstein](6253522.md) on his podcast and pressured Trump's administration to release more information.[^cite_note-Charlie_Kirk_talks_Epstein,_urges_Trump-89] By then, Kirk was one of the most prominent figures in the MAGA movement and often called the face of the movement.[^cite_note-Turning_Point_founder_has_been_a_key_figure-90][^cite_note-Charlie_Kirk_talks_Epstein,_urges_Trump-89] Kirk had insisted that he had no interest in elective office, though stopped saying so in 2025; *The New York Times* reported that "at Turning Point USA, it was widely assumed that the boss would one day be president".[^cite_note-\:1-85][^cite_note-Who_was_Charlie_Kirk?-91]

### Freedom of speech and expression

While Kirk backed Republican crackdowns on the [2024 pro-Palestinian protests on university campuses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_pro-Palestinian_protests_on_university_campuses) and [activist deportations in the second Trump presidency](79561481.md),[^cite_note-TimesofIsrael-92][^cite_note-AlJazeera-93] he opposed crackdowns on pro-Palestinian speech if they were targeted at American citizens. He said: "We've allowed far too many people who hate America move here from abroad, but the right to speak freely is the [birthright](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Birthright) of all Americans."[^cite_note-AlJazeera-93] In April 2025, he expressed concerns that the Trump administration's crackdowns on campuses threatened [free speech](634892.md) and were a [weaponization of antisemitism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Weaponization_of_antisemitism), saying: "Once 'antisemitism' becomes valid grounds to censor or even imprison somebody, there will be frantic efforts to label all kinds of speech as antisemitic — the same way the left labeled all kinds of statements as 'racist' to justify silencing their opposition."[^cite_note-TimesofIsrael-92]

In August 2025, he discussed the burning of [pride flags](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pride_flag), stating: "We should work to overturn every conviction for those arrested, fined, or otherwise harassed for the 'hate crime' of doing [donuts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doughnut_\(driving\)) over Pride flags painted on public streets. It should be legal to burn a rainbow or \[Black Lives Matter] flag in public."[^cite_note-94][^cite_note-95]

### False claims and conspiracy theories



Kirk with [Tucker Carlson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tucker_Carlson) in December 2023

According to *[Forbes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbes)*, Kirk was known for "his repudiation of liberal college education and embrace of [pro-Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pro-Trump) conspiracy theories".[^cite_note-Solender-2020-96] He promoted the [Cultural Marxism conspiracy theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_Marxism_conspiracy_theory),[^cite_note-97][^cite_note-98][^cite_note-snopes_cult_marx-99] and called universities "islands of totalitarianism".[^cite_note-JM-8] In a 2015 speech at the Liberty Forum of Silicon Valley, Kirk said he had applied for nomination to the [U.S. Military Academy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Military_Academy) in [West Point, New York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Point,_New_York), and was not accepted.[^cite_note-Guinto-18] He said that "the slot he considered his went to 'a far less-qualified candidate of a different gender and a different persuasion'" whose test scores he claimed he knew.[^cite_note-JM-8] In 2017 he told *[The New Yorker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_New_Yorker)* that he was being sarcastic when he said it.[^cite_note-JM-8] In 2018 he told the *[Chicago Tribune](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Tribune)* that "he was just repeating something he'd been told",[^cite_note-keilman-2][^cite_note-fn-100] and in October 2019 at a [New Hampshire](21134.md) Turning Point event featuring [Rand Paul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rand_Paul) he claimed he never said it.[^cite_note-fn-100]

Following the [murder of George Floyd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Murder_of_George_Floyd) and subsequent [protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Floyd_protests), Kirk referred to him as a "scumbag", citing his [criminal history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Floyd#Criminal_convictions), and referred to the city of [Minneapolis](6097240.md) (where he was killed) as "not the town it used to be". Kirk promoted debunked claims about [George Floyd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Floyd), such as that he was "illegally counterfeiting currency" and had once "put a gun to a pregnant woman's stomach".[^cite_note-101] On Facebook, YouTube, and Rumble, Kirk repeatedly promoted the false claim that the medical examiner who performed the autopsy declared Floyd had died of an overdose. After a fact check by [Agence France-Presse](471981.md) that noted the doctor stood by the classification of Floyd's death as a homicide, corrections were added to Kirk's posts on social media.[^cite_note-102]

In July 2018, Kirk falsely claimed on social media that [U.S. Justice Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Justice_Department) statistics showed an increase in [human trafficking](19333662.md) arrests from 1,952 in 2016 to 6,087 in the first half of 2018. He deleted the tweet without explanation the next day, after a fact-checker had pointed out that the false 2018 number had originated on the conspiracy site [8chan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/8chan).[^cite_note-103][^cite_note-104] In December 2018, Kirk falsely claimed that protesters in the French [yellow vests movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yellow_vests_movement) chanted "We want Trump". Trump later repeated these false claims.[^cite_note-105]

Ahead of the [2020 U.S. presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_U.S._presidential_election), Kirk spread falsehoods about voter fraud[^cite_note-106][^cite_note-107] and, immediately after Trump lost the 2020 election, Kirk promoted [false and disproven claims of fraud in the election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_lie_\(2020_United_States_presidential_election\)).[^cite_note-108][^cite_note-109] On November 5, 2020, he led a [Stop the Steal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stop_the_Steal) protest at the Maricopa Tabulation Center in Phoenix.[^cite_note-110] Kirk was considered a "big name" social influencer in [Rudy Giuliani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rudy_Giuliani)'s communications plan to [overturn the 2020 election](65905202.md).[^cite_note-111] In August 2025, Kirk called for the elimination of [Jasmine Crockett](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jasmine_Crockett)'s congressional district as a part of the [2025 Texas redistricting](80621571.md), justifying the erasure of her district by claiming she was a part of an "attempt to eliminate the white population in this country".[^cite_note-112]

Shortly before his death, Kirk suggested that [Jeffrey Epstein](6253522.md) had been a [Mossad](27619007.md) agent, with claims of him being "a creation of either Mossad, Israeli intelligence, American intelligence, Saudi intelligence, or maybe he was just a hired gun...". In response, many Israeli government officials, including former Prime Minister [Naftali Bennett](39754840.md), denied Epstein's involvement with them.[^cite_note-MEE2-113][^cite_note-114]

#### COVID-19

In 2020, Kirk spread false information and [conspiracy theories about COVID-19](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conspiracy_theories_about_COVID-19) on [social media](5897742.md). He sharply criticized Democrats' criticism of Trump's withdrawal of WHO funding and called [COVID-19](63030231.md) the "China virus", which Trump retweeted.[^cite_note-gofer-78][^cite_note-abcnewsau-views-115] Kirk alleged that the WHO covered up information about the [COVID-19 pandemic](62750956.md). He was briefly banned from [Twitter](9988187.md) after falsely claiming that [hydroxychloroquine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hydroxychloroquine) had proved to be "100% effective in treating the virus."[^cite_note-gofer-78] He alleged that [Gretchen Whitmer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gretchen_Whitmer), the Democratic governor of Michigan, threatened doctors who tried to use the medication.[^cite_note-gofer-78] [Rudy Giuliani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rudy_Giuliani) retweeted these falsehoods, and Twitter also suspended his account.[^cite_note-gofer-78][^cite_note-116]

In defending the [Trump administration](52231341.md)'s response to [the pandemic](62750956.md), Kirk falsely stated that during the [2009 swine flu pandemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_swine_flu_pandemic) it "took President [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama) 'millions infected and over 1,000 deaths'" to declare a [public health emergency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_health_emergency_\(United_States\)), with the meme shared by Kirk confusing the point at which Trump declared a public health emergency and the point at which Obama issued a [national emergency](298608.md).[^cite_note-Fichera_2020-117][^cite_note-Qiu_&_Bouchard_2020-118] When the [Obama administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Obama_administration) acknowledged the [WHO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WHO)'s declaration of a public health emergency on April 26, 2009, there were fewer than 280 cases of H1N1 infection reported in the U.S., and the first confirmed death (of a Mexican toddler on vacation) occurred the next day, April 27. The WHO projected 1,000,000+ U.S. cases on June 25, after declaring a pandemic on June 11. A spokesman for Turning Point USA acknowledged that its "social media team confused the two different types of emergency declarations", and Trump had not yet issued a national emergency.[^cite_note-Fichera_2020-117][^cite_note-Qiu_&_Bouchard_2020-118]

Kirk described the [public health](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_health) measure of [social distancing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_distancing) prohibitions in churches as a Democratic plot against Christianity and made the unfounded assertion that authorities in [Wuhan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wuhan), China, were burning patients.[^cite_note-gofer-78] In 2020, he said he refused to abide by [mask requirements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Face_masks_during_the_COVID-19_pandemic_in_the_United_States) because "the science around masks is very questionable".[^cite_note-Solender-2020-96][^cite_note-119]

In July 2021, Kirk promoted misleading claims about the efficacy and safety of COVID-19 vaccines.[^cite_note-PostVaccine-27] On the Fox News show hosted by [Tucker Carlson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tucker_Carlson), Kirk called mandatory requirements for students to take the [COVID-19 vaccine](63319438.md) "medical [apartheid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apartheid)".[^cite_note-120] He called for parents to protest at school board meetings, urging them to push back against mask-wearing.[^cite_note-121]

### Social policy

#### Christian nationalism
