Kirk was initially critical of the evangelical right, but he later reversed his position. In 2018, he told [Dave Rubin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dave_Rubin), "We do have a separation of church and state, and we should support that."[^cite_note-Hixenbaugh_&_Smith_2024-43] In 2019, Kirk met Rob McCoy, a pastor of a megachurch in [Ventura County, California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ventura_County,_California), who convinced him that America's founding documents were derived from the Bible.[^cite_note-Hixenbaugh_&_Smith_2024-43] In 2021, Kirk told a congregation, "The Bible says very clearly to 'Occupy until I come'", a verse often cited by followers of the [Seven Mountain Mandate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seven_Mountain_Mandate) to assert that before Jesus returns [evangelical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evangelical) Christians must dominate seven areas of society: government, media, education, business, family, religion, and entertainment. Kirk later interviewed with the creator of the Seven Mountain concept.[^cite_note-Hixenbaugh_&_Smith_2024-43][^cite_note-122][^cite_note-Smith_2025-11][^cite_note-some_are_calling_Charlie_Kirk_a_'martyr'-123] Kirk frequently collaborated with Christian nationalist pastors and preachers, having them as guests on his shows as well as appearing as a speaker at their events,[^cite_note-Clark-124][^cite_note-some_are_calling_Charlie_Kirk_a_'martyr'-123][^cite_note-125] with the [Anti-Defamation League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Defamation_League) accusing Kirk of promoting [Christian nationalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_nationalism).[^cite_note-TimesofIsrael-92]

In 2022, Kirk called the [separation of church and state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Separation_of_church_and_state) in the United States a "fabrication".[^cite_note-Hixenbaugh_&_Smith_2024-43] In 2024, he said, "One of the reasons we're living through a constitutional crisis is that we no longer have a Christian nation, but we have a [Christian form of government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_form_of_government), and they're incompatible. You cannot have liberty if you do not have a Christian population."[^cite_note-126] Appearing at a Trump campaign rally in the same year, he said: "This is a Christian state. I'd like to see it stay that way."[^cite_note-Turning_Point_founder_has_been_a_key_figure-90] By 2024, Kirk's shift to Christian nationalism exemplified its growing approval by the Republican Party under Trump.[^cite_note-Stone_2024-65][^cite_note-Hixenbaugh_&_Smith_2024-43][^cite_note-some_are_calling_Charlie_Kirk_a_'martyr'-123][^cite_note-Post_November_2024-127][^cite_note-Rudnick_2024-66]

Kirk believed in the superiority of the [Western world](21208200.md), credit for which he gave to the [role of Christianity in civilization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Role_of_Christianity_in_civilization). In a 2023 speech, he said that "all men are created equal in the eyes of God, all men and women, but not all cultures are created equal. To say that, you get attacked in every direction, but excuse me when I say that Western civilization is the best that humanity has produced. It's an outgrowth of the Bible."[^cite_note-SpringfieldNL9.11.25-128] Kirk said that Islam was "not compatible with Western civilization".[^cite_note-\:1-85]

#### Abortion

Kirk strongly opposed [abortion](765.md). In a September 2024 debate hosted by [Jubilee Media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jubilee_Media), Kirk argued that [abortion is murder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-abortion_movements) and should be illegal. He opposed exceptions for [rape](7980471.md), including for children as young as 10.[^cite_note-129][^cite_note-130] Kirk compared abortion to [the Holocaust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Holocaust), and said that abortion is worse.[^cite_note-131][^cite_note-abcnewsau-views-115]

#### Gun rights and the Second Amendment

Kirk was a gun owner and [gun rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Right_to_keep_and_bear_arms) advocate. He was opposed to [gun control](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gun_control).[^cite_note-132] After the [Parkland school shooting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parkland_high_school_shooting) in February 2018, he spoke for the [National Rifle Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Rifle_Association) in Parkland, Florida.[^cite_note-Guinto-18][^cite_note-133] Kirk was invited by a student to a pro-gun event in the school where the shooting happened, but the event was canceled. He had said that guns, armed guards, and gun detectors could be used to prevent shootings in schools and campuses.[^cite_note-134][^cite_note-135] In an April 2023 TPUSA event in [Salt Lake City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salt_Lake_City), Utah, Kirk said: "I think it's worth it, I think it's worth to have a cost of, unfortunately, some gun deaths every single year so that we can have the [Second Amendment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Amendment) to protect our other God-given rights."[^cite_note-Snopes2a-136][^cite_note-137][^cite_note-138]

### LGBTQ community

Kirk routinely made [anti-LGBTQ remarks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-LGBTQ_remarks), opposing [transgender rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transgender_rights_in_the_United_States) and medical care, and asserted that there is an "[LGBTQ agenda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBTQ_agenda)".[^cite_note-Coster_&_Tsvetkova_2025-139][^cite_note-Rector_&_Ceballos_2025-140][^cite_note-Hixenbaugh_&_Smith_2024-43] Kirk claimed that being gay was an "error" and likened homosexuality to drug addiction.[^cite_note-141] He also believed monogamous heterosexual marriage should be a prerequisite for adoption.[^cite_note-mmfa-142]

On June 8, 2024, in an episode of his podcast, he criticized YouTuber [Ms. Rachel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ms._Rachel) for a post that celebrated [Pride Month](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pride_Month) by quoting the Bible verse "love thy neighbor", arguing that she was being selective. Kirk told Ms. Rachel, "you might want to crack open that Bible of yours, in a lesser reference — part of the same part of scripture is in [Leviticus 18](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leviticus_18), is that thou shall lay with another man shall be stoned to death. Just saying. So, Ms. Rachel, you quote [Leviticus 19](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leviticus_19), love your neighbor as yourself. The chapter before affirms God's perfect law when it comes to sexual matters."[^cite_note-143][^cite_note-144][^cite_note-145]

#### Same-sex marriage

On November 22, 2019, Kirk said, "I believe marriage is one man, one woman", but added that gay people should be allowed in the conservative movement.[^cite_note-146] In 2022, during an episode of *The Charlie Kirk Show* streamed on YouTube, Kirk criticized the Supreme Court's decision in *[Obergefell v. Hodges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Obergefell_v._Hodges)*. He called LGBTQ activists the "alphabet mafia", claiming that the movement is not "just about two dudes being able to get married".[^cite_note-MMfA-147] Kirk called *Obergefell* a "national takeover of our laws" and argued that conservatives mistakenly thought the issue of [same-sex marriage in the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_the_United_States) would end after the ruling, instead concluding that "...\[gay people] are not happy just having marriage. Instead, they now want to corrupt your children".[^cite_note-MMfA-147]

#### Transgender community

In the op-ed "Sexual Anarchy" for *[The American Mind](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_American_Mind)* on October 14, 2021, Kirk said "the facts are that there are only two genders; that transgenderism and [gender 'fluidity'](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_fluidity) are lies that hurt people and abuse kids."[^cite_note-López_2025-148] In early 2023, he said that transgender women in women's locker rooms should be "taken care of the way we used to take care of things in the 1950s and '60s".[^cite_note-149]

In another 2023 speech, Kirk said, "One issue I think that is so against our senses, so against the [natural law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_law) and, dare I say, a throbbing [middle finger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_finger) to God, is the transgender thing happening in America right now."[^cite_note-Coster_&_Tsvetkova_2025-139] In April 2024, Kirk called for Trump to propose a nationwide ban of [gender-affirming care](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender-affirming_care) for transgender people.[^cite_note-150] That same day, he called for the imprisonment of doctors who perform gender-affirming care and demanded "Nuremberg-style" trials for them.[^cite_note-151] Kirk also actively promoted [misinformation about mass shootings by transgender people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Misinformation_about_violence_by_transgender_people).[^cite_note-Rector_&_Ceballos_2025-140] Statistical evidence shows that such shootings by transgender people are rare.[^cite_note-152]

### Gender roles

Kirk promoted traditional gender roles, telling young women to go to college for the purpose of finding husbands and "embrace their roles as mothers and homemakers".[^cite_note-153] In October 2021, he said on his podcast that Democrats wanted Americans to live where "there is no cultural identity, where you live in sexual anarchy, where private property is a thing of the past, and the ruling class controls everything".[^cite_note-154][^cite_note-155] Following social media backlash, he released a statement on the website of the [Claremont Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Claremont_Institute) reiterating and expanding his remarks.[^cite_note-López_2025-148] According to [Media Matters for America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_Matters_for_America), Kirk said at the TPUSA Young Women's Leadership Summit 2022 Conference that the "biblical model" for women to pursue in romantic relationships is a partner who is "a protector and a leader, and deep down, a vast majority of you agree" and that "if you want to go meet conservative men that have their act together, that aren't like, woke beta men, like, start a Turning Point USA chapter, you'll meet a lot of them."[^cite_note-156] Kirk had repeatedly criticized birth control, and once said that it creates "very angry and bitter young ladies and young women".[^cite_note-157]

### Race

#### White Americans

Kirk had voiced a belief in the decline and victimhood of [White Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_Americans), reflecting [grievance politics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grievance_politics).[^cite_note-158] In 2018, Kirk told a college audience that the concept of [white privilege](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_privilege) is a myth and a "racist idea".[^cite_note-Guinto-18][^cite_note-159] Assuming "more hard-right positions", he told followers of his radio podcast in 2021 that Democratic immigration policies were aimed at "diminishing and decreasing white demographics in America" and called for Texas to "deputize a citizen force and put them on the border" to protect "white demographics in America".[^cite_note-rub-72][^cite_note-160][^cite_note-161]

In 2023, Kirk said that "prowling Blacks go around for fun to go target white people" in urban America.[^cite_note-Charlie_Kirk_in_his_own_words-162] In 2024, he said, "The [great replacement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_genocide_conspiracy_theory) strategy, which is well under way every single day in our southern border, is a strategy to replace white rural America with something different",[^cite_note-Charlie_Kirk_in_his_own_words-162] and added, "The American Democrat party hates this country. They wanna see it collapse. They love it when America becomes less white."[^cite_note-Charlie_Kirk_in_his_own_words-162] Kirk further posted "The '[Great Replacement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Replacement_conspiracy_theory)' is not a theory, it's a reality", alongside a Fox News headline that falsely claimed: "7.2M illegals entered the U.S. under Biden administration, an amount greater than population of 36 states."[^cite_note-163]

#### African Americans

In 2018, Kirk and TPUSA's communication director [Candace Owens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Candace_Owens) presented what the [Southern Poverty Law Center](27164.md) described as "a symphony of racist [dog-whistles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dog_whistle_\(politics\)) ... pandering to the [extreme right](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Far_right)" at the 2018 [Western Conservative Summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Conservative_Summit), with Kirk asserting that the increasing presence of [African Americans in American politics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African_Americans_in_politics) resulted in the Black community being "worse off".[^cite_note-164][^cite_note-165] Kirk cited single motherhood in Chicago's Black community as a cause of [gun violence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gun_violence_in_the_United_States), blaming the absence of a father from some Black households on "a broken culture problem".[^cite_note-166]

Kirk praised Martin Luther King Jr. prior to December 2023, variously calling him a "hero" and a "civil rights icon". That December, he used a speech at [AmericaFest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AmericaFest) to describe him as "awful ... not a good person" and as someone who is admired only because he said "one thing he didn't actually believe".[^cite_note-167] The speech also saw Kirk condemn the [Civil Rights Act of 1964](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_Rights_Act_of_1964), calling its passage a "huge mistake" and alleging that it had created a "permanent [DEI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DEI)-type bureaucracy".[^cite_note-drking-168] Kirk thought the Civil Rights Act of 1964 was a destructive force in American politics that had been turned into an "anti-white weapon".[^cite_note-issues-169][^cite_note-170] Kirk told *The New York Times*, "I take the [Caldwellian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christopher_Caldwell_\(journalist\)) view, from his book *[The Age of Entitlement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Age_of_Entitlement:_America_Since_the_Sixties)*, that we went through a new founding in the '60s and that the Civil Rights Act has actually superseded the U.S. Constitution as its reference point. In fact, I bet if you polled Americans, most of them would have more reverence for the Civil Rights Act than the Constitution. I could be wrong, but I think I'm right."[^cite_note-NYTimesYouthWhisperer-58]

Kirk was a critic of schools and local governments teaching about [racism](2162835.md).[^cite_note-171] He wrote in a 2021 Fox News article that "directly confronting the left, and promising to fight their illiberal ideology with state power when necessary, is the key to winning everyday Americans".[^cite_note-172] He served on Trump's [1776 Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1776_Commission) to advance "patriotic education", which was set up in response to the [1619 Project](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1619_Project).[^cite_note-173] In October 2021, Kirk began the "Exposing Critical Racism Tour" of a number of campuses and off-campus venues to "fight racist theories on America's college campuses!"[^cite_note-174][^cite_note-175] He also opposed [Juneteenth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juneteenth) (a day which commemorates the [end of slavery in the U.S.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/End_of_slavery_in_the_U.S.)) being declared a federal holiday, describing it as "anti-American" for promoting "a neo-segregationist view" that he alleged sought to supplant [Independence Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independence_Day_\(United_States\)).[^cite_note-176]

On July 11, 2023, after the [Supreme Court of the United States](31737.md) ruled that colleges can no longer employ affirmative-action practices in admissions, Representative [Sheila Jackson Lee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sheila_Jackson_Lee) stated on the House floor, "I rise today as a clear recipient of affirmative action, particularly in higher education. I may have been admitted on affirmative action, both in terms of being a woman and a woman of color, but I can declare that I did not graduate on affirmative action." Kirk reacted to this on his podcast on July 13, 2023, by stating, "If we would have said three weeks ago \[...] that [Joy Reid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joy_Reid) and [Michelle Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michelle_Obama) and Sheila Jackson Lee and [Ketanji Brown Jackson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ketanji_Brown_Jackson) were affirmative-action picks, we would have been called racist. But now they're comin' out and they're saying it for us!" He continued, "You do not have the brain processing power to otherwise be taken really seriously. You had to go steal a white person's slot to go be taken somewhat seriously."[^cite_note-177][^cite_note-178]

In January 2024, Kirk said that a "myth" had been created around King which had "grown totally out of control" and that King was currently "the most honored, worshiped, even deified person of the 20th century" despite "most people" supposedly disliking him during his life. Responding to accusations by [Malcolm Kenyatta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malcolm_Kenyatta) that he was working to undermine King and the [Voting Rights Act of 1965](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voting_Rights_Act_of_1965), Kirk called this claim "a lie" and "fear-mongering", and added that telling the "truth" about King "should not be trampling sacred ground" since he was "just a man ... a very flawed one at that" and a "mythological anti-racist creation of the 1960s". Kirk later said he had "found the sacred cow of modern America" in criticizing King.[^cite_note-179] Also in January 2024, Kirk blamed DEI programs for national aviation issues, saying, "If I see a Black pilot, I'm going to be like, 'Boy, I hope he's qualified.'"[^cite_note-180][^cite_note-181][^cite_note-182]

Kirk called Kentanji Brown Jackson a "recipient of affirmative action" on his podcast and said she was nominated for the Supreme Court because of her race, following up with “Biden explicitly said he would only pick a Black woman for the US Supreme Court. Biden himself was an administer of affirmative action."[^cite_note-183] Kirk blamed the high death toll of the [July 2025 Central Texas floods](80354641.md) on DEI.[^cite_note-184] On September 9, 2025, while speaking about the [killing of Iryna Zarutska](80994478.md), Kirk accused Democrats of spreading a "false narrative" that "that there is a relentless assault against Black people on behalf of white people",[^cite_note-185] tweeting "White individuals are actually more likely to be attacked, especially even per capita, by Black individuals in this country." Kirk cited a Fox News video clip in the tweet that reported data from the 2023 [Bureau of Justice statistics.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bureau_of_Justice_Statistics)[^cite_note-Who_was_Charlie_Kirk?-91]

### Relations with Jewish people and Israel

#### Jewish people

In October 2023, Kirk said on *The Charlie Kirk Show* that "Jewish donors have been the number 1 funding mechanism of radical, open border, [neoliberal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neoliberalism), quasi‑[Marxist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marxist) policies ... This is a beast created by secular Jews, and now it's coming for Jews", and also suggested that these Jews control "not just the colleges; it's the nonprofits, it's the movies, it's [Hollywood](418334.md), it's all of it". Soon after, he said that "Jews have been some of the largest funders of [cultural Marxist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_Marxist) ideas and supporters of those ideas over the last 30 or 40 years."[^cite_note-snopes_cult_marx-99] Kirk called on [American Jews](1131183.md) to stop "subsidizing your own demise by supporting institutions that breed Anti-Semites and endorse genocidal killers".[^cite_note-TimesofIsrael-92]

After [Elon Musk](909036.md) endorsed a post which said that "Jewish communities have been pushing the exact kind of hatred against whites that they claim to want people to stop using against them," Kirk defended Musk from charges of [antisemitism](1078.md) by claiming the post's charge against Jewish communities was accurate.[^cite_note-Weisman2024-186][^cite_note-issues-169][^cite_note-Beaumont_2025-3] He went on to claim "the philosophical foundation of anti-whiteness has been largely financed by Jewish donors", but said he was glad that some donors were reconsidering.[^cite_note-187]

In July 2025, Kirk warned his followers against hatred of Jews, calling it "evil" and "demonic".[^cite_note-188] He was quoted as saying that "no non-Jewish person my age has a longer or clearer record of support for [Israel](9282173.md), sympathy with the Jewish people, or opposition to antisemitism than I do".[^cite_note-TimesofIsrael-92] However, Kirk was also accused of [antisemitism](1078.md) by multiple people and organizations;[^cite_note-TimesofIsrael-92][^cite_note-Weisman2024-186][^cite_note-issues-169][^cite_note-Beaumont_2025-3][^cite_note-JPost9.11.25-189] with the [Anti-Defamation League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Defamation_League) accusing Kirk of creating a "vast platform for extremists and far-right conspiracy theorists", a critique of which Kirk rejected.[^cite_note-TimesofIsrael-92]

Days prior to Kirk's assassination, Kirk texted associates on [WhatsApp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WhatsApp) that "Jewish donors play into all the stereotypes" and he felt pushed "\[to] leave the pro-Israel cause" as donors tried to "bully" him by withholding funding for associating with [Candace Owens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Candace_Owens) and [Tucker Carlson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tucker_Carlson), two prominent critics of Israel. That month, businessman [Robert Shillman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Shillman) cancelled a \$2 million donation to TPUSA over Carlson's participation at a TPUSA event. Andrew Kolvet, a spokesperson for TPUSA, reacted to these messages by saying that Kirk's views on Israel were "...complicated and nuanced, and it was a wrestle that was going on for months" and also stated that he believed Kirk was not "turning" on Israel.[^cite_note-190][^cite_note-191]

Some Jewish public figures have defended Kirk against accusations of antisemitism, citing his pro-Israel stance. Some donors to TPUSA are Jewish, including [Bernard Marcus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernard_Marcus).[^cite_note-192] [Dennis Prager](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dennis_Prager), the Jewish co-founder of [PragerU](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PragerU), was quoted, "To call Charlie Kirk an antisemite — and further, to say he's long been accused of being such — is to so cheapen the word as to render it meaningless."[^cite_note-Weisman2024-186]

#### Government of Israel

Kirk was highly supportive of Israel.[^cite_note-auto-193] During a 2019 visit to [Jerusalem](16043.md), he told an audience "I'm very [pro-Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pro-Israel) ... and my whole life I have defended Israel".[^cite_note-TimesofIsrael-92] In August 2025, he said "I have a bulletproof resumé showing my defense of Israel ... I believe in the scriptural land rights given to Israel. I believe in [fulfilment of prophecy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_Zionism)", and added that he would "fight for" Israel.[^cite_note-MEE2-113]

In September 2025, conservative political commentator [Tucker Carlson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tucker_Carlson) claimed that Kirk loved Israel, but disliked Israeli Prime Minister [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) and was "appalled by what was happening in Gaza." Carlson also claimed that Kirk believed Netanyahu was using the United States to wage wars on behalf of Israel.[^cite_note-194][^cite_note-196]

In December 2025, *[The Jerusalem Post](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Jerusalem_Post)* reported that Kirk had sent a letter to Israeli Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu warning that the country was "losing the [information war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Information_warfare)" among young Americans. According to the report, he argued that reliance on American pro-Israel advocates was insufficient and that pro-Palestinian narratives were being spread. Kirk urged Israel to develop a direct, independent voice to communicate its perspective.[^cite_note-JPostReforms-197] Elements of his recommendations were subsequently considered in Israel's adoption of new messaging reforms, including multilingual digital content and the use of personal testimonies to reach international audiences.[^cite_note-JPostReforms-197]

Several Israeli government ministers, politicians, and political activists mourned Kirk's death, with many describing him as a "friend of Israel" and a few linking his killing to [anti-Zionists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Zionists).[^cite_note-JPost9.11.25-189] Israel's Foreign Minister [Gideon Sa'ar](485357.md) described Kirk as a representative of "the [Judeo-Christian values](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judeo-Christian_ethics) that unite Israel and America" and as a "fearless warrior for truth and freedom".[^cite_note-AlJazeera-93] Netanyahu said he had invited Kirk to Israel prior to Kirk's death, while [Morton Klein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morton_Klein) said Kirk had recently accepted an invitation to speak at the [Zionist Organization of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zionist_Organization_of_America)'s national gala.[^cite_note-TimesofIsrael-92]

### Islam

In 2018, Kirk was a speaker for the annual conference held by the anti-Muslim advocacy group [ACT for America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ACT_for_America) in [Washington DC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_DC).[^cite_note-198]

In 2025, Kirk wrote on Twitter that "Islam is the sword the left is using to slit the throat of America."[^cite_note-AlJazeera-93] Following the victory of [Zohran Mamdani](64618347.md) in the [2025 New York City Democratic mayoral primary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_New_York_City_Democratic_mayoral_primary), Kirk posted that "24 years ago a group of Muslims killed 2,753 people on 9/11. Now a Muslim Socialist is on pace to run New York City." Liberal Fox News commentator [Jessica Tarlov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jessica_Tarlov) asked Kirk to take down the "gross and [Islamophobic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamophobic)" post.[^cite_note-199] In a separate post, Kirk argued that "It's not Islamophobia to notice that Muslims want to import values into the West that seek to destabilize our civilization."[^cite_note-200]

### Immigration and deportation

Kirk's speech at the [United States Capitol](31979.md) following the [second inauguration of Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_inauguration_of_Donald_Trump) on January 20, 2025

At a 2023 event at Missouri State University, Kirk said that [immigration to the United States](15051.md) should be completely stopped.[^cite_note-SpringfieldNL9.11.25-128] In the lead-up to the 2024 presidential election, Kirk promoted the false claim that Haitians in Springfield, Ohio, [were eating residents' pets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Springfield_pet-eating_hoax) and other wildlife.[^cite_note-201] Kirk called for the use of force against migrants at the U.S.–Mexico border, including the use of tear gas, rubber bullets, and whips. Kirk said that migrants were "bringing force upon themselves" by "invading" the country. In justifying this use of force, Kirk promoted false claims of disproportionate criminality among migrants, saying: "Those are the men that will go into your communities and break into your homes and rape your women, take your children. But, hey, they're – they're [dreamers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DREAM_Act)."[^cite_note-202]

In 2023, Kirk called for [Mehdi Hasan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mehdi_Hasan) to be deported and deplatformed over his views on the COVID-19 pandemic, calling him a "neurotic lunatic" and saying, "Send him back to the country he came from. Holy cow! Get him off TV. Revoke his visa."[^cite_note-203] In October 2023, Kirk also called U.S. representative [Ilhan Omar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ilhan_Omar) a "terrorist sympathizer" and called for her deportation.[^cite_note-SpringfieldNL9.11.25-128]

#### Indian immigration visas

Kirk was vocal about his disapproval of [immigration](15051.md) of [Indians](7564733.md), particularly non-Christian Indians, into the US. Kirk stated that "America does not need more visas for people from India", arguing that Indian immigrants take job opportunities from Americans.[^cite_note-GuardianAbroad-204] Separately, Kirk stated that America would still be America if 90% of the population were of Indian national origin, as long as they were [Christian Indians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_Indians).[^cite_note-w283-205]

### Opioid epidemic

Kirk blamed the Chinese government and [drug cartels](197015.md) for the [opioid crisis in the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opioid_crisis_in_the_United_States), telling the audience that "almost nobody in this audience has a friend that you've lost to the Russian government but you do have a friend or a family member that has died because of the cartels and the [Chinese Communist Party](7175.md) with a [fentanyl](141915.md) \[*[sic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sic)*] coming into our communities".[^cite_note-RBC-Ukraine-206]

### Foreign affairs

#### Gaza War and Twelve-Day War

Kirk often repeated [pro-Israeli talking points](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_public_diplomacy_in_the_Gaza_war) about the ongoing [Gaza War](74998519.md).[^cite_note-MEE2-113] He blamed [Hamas](13913.md) for the deaths of civilians in Gaza,[^cite_note-MEE2-113] and denied that Israel is [starving Palestinians](75828824.md).[^cite_note-TimesofIsrael-92] Kirk said of Palestine, "I don't think the place exists."[^cite_note-zeteo-charlie-kirk-in-his-own-words-207]

Shortly after the [October 7 attacks](74998317.md) on Israel in 2023, Kirk promoted a conspiracy theory alleging the Israeli government knew that Hamas was going to launch the attack, and that [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) allowed it to go ahead as part of a plan to remain in power.[^cite_note-Novak-2023-195] In May 2025, Kirk opposed a [bipartisan](70001288.md) bill to expand [anti-BDS laws](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-BDS_laws), which punish the [boycott of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boycott_of_Israel).[^cite_note-208] He said the bill would "only create more antisemitism, and play into growing narratives that Israel is running the U.S. government".[^cite_note-209]

Kirk opposed U.S. involvement in the [Twelve-Day War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twelve-Day_War),[^cite_note-kirk-iran-210] warning that a prolonged war would destabilize the region and could trigger a [refugee](45547.md) crisis and [civil war](7085.md) in Iran, though he maintained "full and complete trust" in Trump.[^cite_note-kirk-iran-210]

#### Russian invasion of Ukraine

Kirk often advanced [pro-Russian talking points](70144390.md) about the [Russo-Ukrainian War](42085878.md).[^cite_note-KyivPost-211] In the days before the [Russian invasion of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine), Kirk characterized the [tensions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prelude_to_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine) as a "border dispute" and repeated false claims from Russian state media that Ukrainian forces had been shelling a [Russian separatist enclave](42425747.md). Kirk's spokesman said at the time that while Kirk disagreed with the Russian invasion, he was "rightly questioning" [U.S. foreign policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._foreign_policy).[^cite_note-212]

Kirk opposed the U.S. sending arms to Ukraine or providing financial support to the country.[^cite_note-KyivPost-211] In August 2025, Kirk disagreed with Trump's decision to send more [military aid to Ukraine](70376708.md), saying: "We were against it with Biden. Why would we be for it now? Unless it gets us to a [peace settlement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peace_negotiations_in_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine)".[^cite_note-213] He called Ukrainian president [Volodymyr Zelenskyy](59210523.md) a "CIA puppet" and "gangster" who "sent his own people to a senseless massacre",[^cite_note-24Kanal-214] claiming that Zelenskyy had no interest in ending the war.[^cite_note-RBC-Ukraine-206] Kirk said that Ukraine should cut spending on what he called a war it could not "win".[^cite_note-24Kanal-214] He also claimed that [Crimea](163045.md) could not be returned to Ukraine because "it has always been part of Russia".[^cite_note-24Kanal-214]

In November 2024, Kirk offered an "apology" to the Russian people, stating "very few Americans want war with you" and that "the people obsessed with fighting you forever" were a minority "on their way out of power". His post was shared by the Russian state-owned news agency [RT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RT_\(TV_network\)).[^cite_note-215] Kirk believed that the U.S. was "wrong" to view Russia as an enemy, although he said he did not like "the Russian Federation or Russian dictator [Vladimir Putin](32817.md)".[^cite_note-KyivPost-211] At the February 2022 [Conservative Political Action Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservative_Political_Action_Conference), Kirk said that "the [southern border](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexico%E2%80%93United_States_border) matters a lot more than the [Ukrainian border](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_border)" and "I want every Republican leader ... to call what's happening on the southern border an invasion because two million people waltzed into our country last year."[^cite_note-216]

#### Greenland

In January 2025, Kirk and [Donald Trump, Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump,_Jr.) visited [Greenland](12118.md) and hosted a lunch for locals in an effort to build support for a [US annexation of Greenland](82044223.md). Local journalists later discovered that the group included homeless people recruited with the promise of a free meal.[^cite_note-217] After his return to the US, Kirk said on his podcast that the young people of Greenland considered annexation to be a matter of dignity and that they wanted "to be part of a country that respects them, that gives them human rights".[^cite_note-ludicrous-218] Following [Greenland's parliamentary elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Greenlandic_general_election) in March, in which voters repudiated the idea of annexation, he said that the "election shows that the pro-American forces are ascendant in Greenland, and it should be partnered with America, not the Old World."[^cite_note-ludicrous-218]

#### China and Taiwan

Kirk told his listeners in 2025, "I would say, sadly, if we took [Taiwan](25734.md), it would probably start a nuclear war. Our leaders have largely mishandled [China](5405.md). We probably should have taken \[Taiwan] in 1950 right after [World War II](32927.md)."[^cite_note-GuardianAbroad-204]

#### South Korea and Japan

The week before he was killed, Kirk made stops at [Seoul](19159283.md) and [Tokyo](30057.md) to spread his conservative message across Asia. In Seoul, he spoke to an event hosted by [Mina Kim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mina_Kim)'s Build Up Korea, where he spoke against immigration and communism, and cheered the prevalence of [Christianity in South Korea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianity_in_South_Korea). In Tokyo, he spoke at an event hosted by [Sanseitō](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanseit%C5%8D), a right-wing populist [political party in Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_political_parties_in_Japan) whose leader [Sohei Kamiya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sohei_Kamiya) described the party's ideology as equivalent to Trumpism. Kirk spoke out against [migrant workers in Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Migrant_workers_in_Japan) and said that he was "thrilled" to see a "growing political movement" against [globalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Globalism).[^cite_note-219]

### Climate change

Kirk opposed the [2016 Paris Agreement](30242372.md) on [climate change](5042951.md).[^cite_note-DeSmog-220] Kirk promoted [climate change denial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_change_denial), calling [global warming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_warming) a hoax.[^cite_note-221] In 2017, Kirk admitted that TPUSA had accepted [funding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fossil_fuels_lobby) from the [fossil fuel industry](994887.md). He spoke out against targeting [fossil fuels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fossil_fuel) and opposed student campaigns that pressured universities to [divest from fossil fuels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fossil_fuel_divestment).[^cite_note-JM-8][^cite_note-DeSmog-220] In 2021, a Turning Point USA video featuring Kirk and Candace Owens claimed there is "no factual data to back up global warming" and that scientists do not know the cause; Science Feedback rated the claims inaccurate. Kirk later issued a correction, and the video was removed.[^cite_note-auto2-222] In 2022, Kirk warned that [climate activism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_activism) would erode American sovereignty and private property, describing it as a [Trojan Horse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trojan_Horse) for [Marxism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marxism) and likening it to "pseudo-[paganism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paganism)". He called the statement that climate change is an [existential threat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_catastrophe_scenarios) "complete gibberish nonsense", stating that if your biggest worry in life is existential, you have a great life, and added that he did not believe human activity is the driver of climate change.[^cite_note-223]

## Personal life



Kirk and his wife, [Erika](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erika_Kirk), speaking together at an event in Texas in June 2025

In May 2021, Kirk married [Erika Kirk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erika_Kirk) ([née](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Birth_name#Maiden_and_married_names) Frantzve), a businesswoman and podcaster who won the [Miss Arizona USA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miss_Arizona_USA) pageant competition in 2012.[^cite_note-224][^cite_note-225] The couple have a daughter, born in August 2022,[^cite_note-226][^cite_note-auto6-227] and a son, born in May 2024.[^cite_note-auto6-227]

Kirk's real estate portfolio consisted of three properties, including a \$4.75 million estate in Scottsdale, Arizona,[^cite_note-228][^cite_note-229] and a beachside condominium on the [Florida Gulf Coast](11969.md) purchased for \$855,000.[^cite_note-k748-230][^cite_note-Conservative_leader_Charlie_Kirk_shot-231]

### Religious views

Kirk was an [evangelical Christian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evangelicalism_in_the_United_States),[^cite_note-232] belonging to the [Calvary Chapel Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calvary_Chapel_Association).[^cite_note-Potts2024-48] Prior to the early 2020s, Kirk was described as secular and a critic of religious influence on politics and the state.[^cite_note-Hixenbaugh_&_Smith_2024-43][^cite_note-233] He later became a [Christian nationalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_nationalist). In 2021, Kirk partnered with California pastor Rob McCoy to launch TPUSA Faith to mobilize conservative Christians to vote Republican. Kirk's shift was influenced by events such as [Trump's move of the American embassy in Israel to Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trump's_move_of_the_American_embassy_in_Israel_to_Jerusalem) and COVID-era church closures, which he and his allies portrayed as religious persecution.[^cite_note-Hixenbaugh_&_Smith_2024-43] In January 2025 he said that he had been keeping a "Jewish sabbath" since 2021, turning off his phone from Friday night to Saturday night, considering it to be a Christian commandment.[^cite_note-sabbath-234]

Kirk advocated Christian [creationism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Creationism), arguing that [evolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evolution) is false and that [Charles Darwin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Darwin) has been debunked. He has discussed with Randy Guliuzza, the president of the [Institute for Creation Research](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institute_for_Creation_Research), ICR's support for [young Earth creationism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Young_Earth_creationism) on his podcast.[^cite_note-235] Kirk's [YouTube](3524766.md) page includes footage of informal debate on this topic at his signature "Prove Me Wrong" table on campus.[^cite_note-236] Speaking on a podcast episode with creationist [Stephen Meyer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_Meyer), Kirk said he was intrigued by Meyer's argument that there was scientific confirmation for [intelligent design](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intelligent_design), contrary to Darwin.[^cite_note-237]

Kirk elaborated on [Hinduism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism) and his disapproval of its morality due to its [polytheism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polytheism), stating: "When you have multiple gods, you get different moralities. And the West has largely embraced the idea that there is a standard of conduct, or a best way to live."[^cite_note-Seely_2025-238] Furthermore, in reply to an inquiry about how that claim was not inclusive of other religious worldviews, he responded: "I don't seek to be inclusive, I seek what is best. And the [Ten Commandments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ten_Commandments) are what is best. Would it be offensive to a young Hindu kid? Maybe, maybe not. But it also is a reminder they're living in a country that's a [monotheistic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monotheistic) country."[^cite_note-Seely_2025-238]

## Assassination

Main article: [Assassination of Charlie Kirk](81040139.md)



The tent in which Kirk was fatally shot, cordoned off with police tape

On September 10, 2025, while on stage at [Utah Valley University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Utah_Valley_University) in [Orem, Utah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orem,_Utah), for a TPUSA event, "The American Comeback Tour",[^cite_note-239][^cite_note-240] Kirk was fatally shot in the neck. The shooting took place at 12\:23 p.m. [MDT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mountain_Time_Zone) (18\:23 UTC), around 20 minutes after the event began, in front of an audience of about 3,000 people.[^cite_note-241][^cite_note-242][^cite_note-bbcShooting-243] Kirk was taken to [Timpanogos Regional Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timpanogos_Regional_Hospital) in [critical condition](30864674.md), where he was pronounced dead later that afternoon.[^cite_note-244][^cite_note-auto5-245]

Authorities arrested the suspected shooter, 22-year-old [Tyler James Robinson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tyler_James_Robinson), in [Washington, Utah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington,_Utah), on September 12.[^cite_note-246] Four days later, on September 16, he was charged with [aggravated murder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aggravated_murder) and other offenses.[^cite_note-247]

### Public vigil and memorial service

Main article: [Memorial service of Charlie Kirk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memorial_service_of_Charlie_Kirk)

On September 14, a public [vigil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vigil) was held for Kirk at [John F. Kennedy Center for the Performing Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_F._Kennedy_Center_for_the_Performing_Arts), and was attended by Trump administration officials and Republican lawmakers, including House speaker [Mike Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Johnson), White House press secretary [Karoline Leavitt](71739774.md), and Health and Human Services Secretary [Robert F. Kennedy Jr.](522298.md)[^cite_note-248]

A [memorial service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memorial_service_of_Charlie_Kirk) followed on September 21 at [State Farm Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Farm_Stadium) in [Glendale, Arizona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glendale,_Arizona), which reached full capacity with a total turnout nearing 100,000 people.[^cite_note-249] Prominent figures in attendance included President Trump, Vice President [JD Vance](53396477.md), Kennedy Jr., and Defense Secretary [Pete Hegseth](34076202.md). The list of speakers included political commentator [Tucker Carlson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tucker_Carlson), White House chief of staff [Susie Wiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Susie_Wiles), and Kirk's widow Erika,[^cite_note-250] who publicly forgave Robinson during her remarks.[^cite_note-251]

### Reactions

Main article: [Assassination of Charlie Kirk § Reactions and analysis](81040139.md#Reactions_and_analysis)

President Trump addressing the nation from the [Oval Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oval_Office) about the shooting on September 10, 2025

Following the shooting and before Kirk was pronounced dead, Trump called for prayers for him on [Truth Social](69077351.md).[^cite_note-auto5-245][^cite_note-NYTshooting-252] Several prominent political figures from both parties, including all living former presidents ([Bill Clinton](3356.md), [George W. Bush](3414021.md), Barack Obama, and Joe Biden), echoed the sentiment,[^cite_note-bbcShooting-243][^cite_note-auto5-245] as did a number of international heads of governments,[^cite_note-JPost9.11.25-189] among other officials.[^cite_note-253][^cite_note-254][^cite_note-255] Internationally, several vigils were held in honor of Kirk outside of the local U.S. embassies.[^cite_note-256] The vigil in Vienna was attended by the [youth wing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Youth_wing) of the far-right [Freedom Party of Austria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_Party_of_Austria), as well as far-right activist [Martin Sellner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Sellner).[^cite_note-257]

The American right demanded severe penalties for the individuals responsible for the assassination of Kirk. [Steve Bannon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steve_Bannon), who previously served as an adviser to Trump, has advocated for widespread arrests and a stringent response towards universities. In the meantime, Hegseth instructed his staff to identify and discipline service members who either mocked or expressed approval of Kirk's murder.[^cite_note-258]

In the days after Kirk's death, Americans were equally likely to have a favorable or an unfavorable opinion of him, with many having no opinion.[^cite_note-259] Despite divided public sentiment, commentators and political allies have described Kirk as a major figure in the contemporary American right,[^cite_note-260] citing his influence on youth activism, Christian nationalism, and the MAGA movement.[^cite_note-261][^cite_note-262] Some politicians responded to the shooting by linking it to broader political debates. Republicans have accused liberals of "inciting violence with rhetoric", while Democrats have used the event to further discussions of [gun control](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gun_politics_in_the_United_States) legislation.[^cite_note-Allen-Terkel-263] Trump and congressional Republicans received criticism for immediately blaming Democrats and liberal beliefs for the shooting, but without evidence,[^cite_note-264][^cite_note-265] drawing allegations of exploiting the death for political gain.[^cite_note-Peters_09192025-266][^cite_note-267] Responses of students and professors at universities to Kirk's death varied, including both outright celebrations of his death and vigils in his honor.[^cite_note-268][^cite_note-269][^cite_note-270]

### Aftermath

Main article: [Reprisals against commentators on the Charlie Kirk assassination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reprisals_against_commentators_on_the_Charlie_Kirk_assassination)



President [Donald Trump](4848272.md) embracing [Erika Kirk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erika_Kirk) at her husband's memorial on September 21, 2025

[Far-right](221220.md) activists called for violence and retaliation in the aftermath of Kirk's assassination, with [Laura Loomer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laura_Loomer) saying "The Left are terrorists."[^cite_note-271] and [doxxed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doxxed) people they accused of celebrating or justifying Kirk's death.[^cite_note-272] Right-wing activists and members of the Trump administration's initial demands—that people allegedly celebrating Kirk's death be silenced and fired—soon evolved into a campaign to punish people who expressed criticism of Kirk.[^cite_note-Riccardi_09152025-273] The administration's involvement led to comparisons with [McCarthyism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McCarthyism) and [cancel culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cancel_culture);[^cite_note-Riccardi_09152025-273] *The New York Times* called it "a conservative version of the cancel culture that only a few years ago was wielded by the American left",[^cite_note-274][^cite_note-Peters_09192025-266] and said it was evidence of the rise of a "[woke right](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woke_right)".[^cite_note-Peters_09192025-266] A *[USA Today](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USA_Today)* analysis showed that by September 18 more than 100 people—including lawyers, doctors, first responders, and more than 50 high school teachers and college professors—had been censured, suspended, dismissed, or were under investigation.[^cite_note-275] In response to the conservative and government campaign to silence critics of Kirk, especially following the [suspension of comedian Jimmy Kimmel's show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suspension_of_Jimmy_Kimmel_Live!), various commentators and publications discussed the issues of cancel culture and [free speech in the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_speech_in_the_United_States) in the aftermath of Kirk's death, including among others Tucker Carlson,[^cite_note-276] *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)*,[^cite_note-277] NBC News,[^cite_note-278] [Reuters](18998750.md),[^cite_note-279] and *USA Today*.[^cite_note-280] Natasha Lindstaedt, a professor in the Department of Government at the [University of Essex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Essex), wrote that some scholars have argued that the characterization of Kirk as a political martyr is "part of a Trump administration campaign to vilify the liberal left."[^cite_note-281]

## Legacy and recognition

Trump posthumously awarding Kirk the [Presidential Medal of Freedom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidential_Medal_of_Freedom) on October 14, 2025

In 2018, Kirk was listed on that year's [*Forbes* 30 Under 30](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbes_30_Under_30) under Law & Policy.[^cite_note-282][^cite_note-283] In May 2019, he was awarded the [honorary degree](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Honorary_degree) of Doctor of Humanities (D.Hum.) from [Liberty University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberty_University).[^cite_note-284]

On September 10, 2025, following Kirk's assassination, President Trump ordered that the [flag of the United States](11447.md) be flown at half-staff at the White House, on all public buildings and grounds, at all military posts and naval stations, and on all naval vessels of the federal government in the District of Columbia and throughout the United States and its territories and possessions, as a mark of respect for Kirk. The order remained in effect until sunset on September 14.[^cite_note-y820-285]

On September 11, President Trump announced that Kirk would posthumously be awarded the Presidential Medal of Freedom.[^cite_note-286] He formally bestowed the award on October 14. His widow, Erika, accepted it on his behalf.[^cite_note-287] On September 13, the mayor of the city of [Netanya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netanya), Israel, announced that a traffic circle in the city would be renamed in honor of Kirk.[^cite_note-288] At Kirk's memorial service on September 21, [Hillsdale College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hillsdale_College) president [Larry P. Arnn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Larry_P._Arnn) announced that he would be posthumously awarded an [honorary degree](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Honorary_degree).[^cite_note-289] On November 12, the [city council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_council) of [Westminster, California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Westminster,_California), partially renamed a street in the city to Charlie Kirk Way.[^cite_note-Fry_2025-290]

On September 16, the commemorative [AI-generated song](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_and_artificial_intelligence) "[We Are Charlie Kirk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/We_Are_Charlie_Kirk)" was released, becoming one of the first AI works to ever list on *[Billboard](18309966.md)* and [Spotify](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spotify)'s viral song [charts](1795886.md).[^cite_note-291] In March 2026, the [Department of Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Department_of_Education) hung banners honoring [Booker T. Washington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Booker_T._Washington), [Catharine Beecher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catharine_Beecher), and Kirk as American heroes.[^cite_note-292]

In September 2026, on the one-year anniversary of Kirk's assassination, an eight foot bronze statue of Kirk was unveiled at the TPUSA headquarters in Phoenix. It was temporarily placed outside of the TPUSA facility, with plans to bring it inside at the end of the week for security purposes.[^cite_note-293] The day after the statue was unveiled, it was vandalized with red paint smeared across the neck, face, and torso, as was a nearby Christian memorial poster. In response, Kirk's widow Erika stated that the damages would be repaired and that it would have no impact on his legacy, and the statue was promptly cleaned.[^cite_note-294][^cite_note-295][^cite_note-296] Another statue of Kirk, called [*Freedom*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_\(statue\)), was built and unveiled on the one-year anniversary as well and placed in [Times Square](31389.md).[^cite_note-297]

Daily production of *The Charlie Kirk Show* continued despite Kirk's death.[^cite_note-\:1-85]

## Books

Kirk was the author of several books.[^cite_note-Hindustan_Times_2025-298] Along with Brent Hamachek, he co-wrote the 2016 book *Time for a Turning Point: Setting a Course Toward Free Markets and Limited Government for Future Generations*,[^cite_note-Hindustan_Times_2025-298] which was published by [Post Hill Press](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Post_Hill_Press), a subsidiary of [Simon & Schuster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simon_&_Schuster).[^cite_note-Murray_2025-299] Under the same publisher, Kirk wrote the 2018 book *Campus Battlefield: How Conservatives Can WIN the Battle on Campus and Why It Matters*.[^cite_note-Hindustan_Times_2025-298][^cite_note-Murray_2025-299] Donald Trump Jr. wrote the foreword for the book.[^cite_note-Hindustan_Times_2025-298][^cite_note-Murray_2025-299] In a review for *[The Weekly Standard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Weekly_Standard)*, Adam Rubenstein described the book as a "hot mess", "nothing more than a marketing pitch for TPUSA", and said the "thin" book was "stuffed with reprintings of his tweets and quotes from others".[^cite_note-300]

In 2020, Kirk wrote *The MAGA Doctrine: The Only Ideas That Will Win the Future*, which was published by [HarperCollins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HarperCollins).[^cite_note-Hindustan_Times_2025-298][^cite_note-Murray_2025-299] In its review for *[The New York Times](30680.md)*, Gabriel Debenedetti wrote that "Kirk's musing about whether '[The Art of The Deal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Art_of_the_Deal)' might one day be considered a 'religious tract' comes just nine chapters after the book highlights the importance of 'a healthy dose of skepticism about authority figures and experts who think they knew best.' And that's just pages after its dedication to Donald Trump, which is five chapters before Kirk wonders whether Trump might 'be remembered as the president who brought about world peace.'"[^cite_note-301] In *Open Letters Review*, Steve Donoghue said of the book that "On every page, Kirk writes something that's either trivially, casually wrong ... or just bipartisanly ridiculous".[^cite_note-302]

In 2022, *The College Scam: How America's Universities Are Bankrupting and Brainwashing Away the Future of America's Youth* was published.[^cite_note-303] In 2024, *Right Wing Revolution: How to Beat the Woke and Save the West* was released. In the book, Kirk argues that the United States is "under threat from a lethal ideology that seeks to humiliate and erase anyone that does not bow at its altar".[^cite_note-304] His last two books were both released by Winning Team Publishing, a conservative publishing house co-founded by Trump Jr.[^cite_note-Hindustan_Times_2025-298][^cite_note-Murray_2025-299] A book written by Kirk, titled *Stop, in the Name of God: Why Honoring the Sabbath Will Transform Your Life*, was released by Winning Team Publishing on December 9, 2025.[^cite_note-305]

'We do have a separation of church and state,' Kirk told the conservative commentator Dave Rubin in 2018, 'and we should support that.' Kirk, now 30, has since reversed his position.
